## #236 root 1 — content-based replay recognition (owner-task apply crash-window) — MERGED (PR #247, squash `1a33b99`). #236 stays OPEN (roots 2/3 remain). Fourth of the 9h-precondition backlog (PARTIAL: root 1 only).

Dark mechanism repair (operator decision via AskUserQuestion: build dark now / **option 1 content-based recognition** / **root 1 only** / #245 stays deferred). The crash window: `task-apply` pushes with `--force-with-lease` then `finalize` posts the authenticated bot marker; if the job dies after the push and before the marker, a whole-workflow replay re-enters `decide`, which hits head-freshness FIRST and posts a FALSE stale-notice (the applied commit is now the head, parent==reviewed-head R) — the duplicate then materialises via the owner re-issuing (`--force-with-lease` blocks a same-run re-push, so it is NOT a same-run re-admission — a framing the planner corrected against live code). The fix: a `findContentAppliedCommit` helper at the **`beforeAnalysis` stale-notice gate (`apply-owner-task.mts:938`)** recognises an already-applied commit from PR compare history by byte-exact (parent == reviewed head, tree == deterministic apply tree) and terminates as the existing silent `no-op-success`. Durable (git history) + content-authenticated (the tree oid pins content, so authorship is irrelevant — this is NOT a re-promotion of the D148-demoted forgeable trailer, which stays hardcoded false); strictly subtractive on writes; fail-closed (the full compare response — every entry, every parent — is validated before any recognition). Codex-MCP authored every fold (thread `019ff087`); fsr(opus) x4 + local codex x5 + the cloud connector formed the floor. **0 post-open rounds** — connector CLEAN first try. Non-activating: no enable var, no credential, no new permission/execution class; `FACTORY_PAUSED` stays `true`. #236 stays a hard 9h precondition (count still three: #236/#237/#245).

### Learning 75 — the orchestrator's contract re-verification must check control-FLOW reachability, not just that cited file:line anchors exist; a dead guard passes gates and coverage and is only caught by flow reasoning at the spec stage.
The story-planner's first draft anchored the recognition interception at `decide:950` (after the final `outcome` is computed). Re-deriving the live control flow showed that the crash-window stale-notice is produced by `beforeAnalysis` at `:933` (called with a deliberately-unread analysis) and returns at the `:938-948` gate — so `:950` is *unreachable* for the stale case, exactly the dead-guard failure mode the planner itself had warned about, relocated. Had this reached Codex, it would have shipped a guard that passes lint/typecheck/coverage (it is simply never exercised on the intended path) and only an fsr/connector round — or worse, a live 9h duplicate — would have surfaced it. The topology-v2 rule "the orchestrator verifies each file:line citation vs live code" is load-bearing precisely because the expensive spec defects are not missing citations (those are cheap to catch) but citations that exist yet sit off the reachable path. The fix was a cheap planner reissue (fable) against a precise flow trace, pre-implementation — the shift-left of shift-left. General rule: when verifying a contract's insertion point, trace the actual branch/return sequence that produces the target state, don't just confirm the named symbols are present near the cited lines.

### Learning 76 — fold-vs-defer for a diverse-lens finding: fold genuinely-new fail-opens AND categorical class-closers pre-open; defer an availability-only cross-cutting edge to the unit that owns its family — and the reward for closing the class pre-open is zero post-open rounds.
Six findings folded pre-open. F1/F2/F4 were test-quality #160 gaps (a fail-closed guard whose removal survived because a negative test's assertion was too loose — a coincidental downstream `TypeError` masked the dropped guard); F3 corrected a real availability regression (a valid merge commit crashed the job instead of being skipped as a non-candidate). F5 then F6 were the same class surfaced twice by the local-codex diverse lens: "validate all evidence before accepting a recognition" — first for trailing entries after a match, then for the parent objects inside a skipped multi-parent commit. Rather than patch each instance, the fold wrote the **general rule** (a two-phase per-entry loop: fully validate every field of every entry, INCLUDING every parent object, before ANY classify/skip/match/return), which fsr confirmed CLOSED the class — no reachable unvalidated-field path remains. That is L70's "write the general rule to end the tail" applied at the code level: the categorical fix TERMINATES the connector's next-instance discovery rather than perpetuating it. F7 — a head-freshness TOCTOU (force-push race → silent no-op vs stale-notice) — was a *different* class but was **deferred**, not folded, on the direction test: it is availability-only (recognition is strictly subtractive, and #238's applied-head re-validation gates the human merge against the live head), it is NOT introduced here (the sibling marker-replay and finalize reachability paths share the identical fetched-snapshot pattern), and its categorical fix spans that whole family → it belongs to #236's roots 2/3, the unit that owns the full outcome-record design; folding only the new path would make root 1 inconsistently stricter than its siblings. The hard cap held (fold only genuinely-new fail-opens; a same-class categorical closer counts as terminating not tail-chasing; defer availability-only family-wide edges with in-thread rationale). The payoff was direct: because the evidence-validation class was closed pre-open, the connector's post-open auto-review came back CLEAN first try (0 post-open rounds), and the whole review cost stayed in the pre-open floor (shift-left / D103).

**Process note.** Operator decisions via AskUserQuestion set the shape (dark-buildable-vs-reclassify + root-1-only scope + #245 fold-vs-defer). Implementer Codex-MCP (D145 default; 1 implement + 3 fold-replies). Floor: fsr(opus) 4 passes all CONFIRMED-SOUND (initial + a micro-recheck on each of the 3 code-logic folds), local `codex review` 5 passes (surfaced F5 then F6 — the diverse lens doing exactly its claim-accuracy job), gates green every round (`apply-owner-task.mts` 100% stmts/funcs/lines, 99.13% branch; 3865 tests). Connector auto-review CLEAN first try (👀 → 👍, post-`ready_for_review`, head-matched, no findings review). claude-review + spec-grounded both SUCCESS post-ready (claude-review metadata-only per #204/#211, self-disclosing its limitation — not a finding). pr-triage (D23) MERGEABLE after independently re-reading the code, closingIssuesReferences (exactly `[]` — closes neither #236 nor #9), the connector chronology, and the code-scanning channel; it correctly flagged that it could not re-derive the GC'd transient 👀 post-hoc, covered by the orchestrator's own first-hand poll capture of the bot 👀 preceding the 👍. Squash-merged on delegated authority; `closingIssuesReferences` held `[]` throughout (#236/#9 stayed open). Open→merge 16m45s. Registry note rode the PR (D135 conventional path, author `syamaner`), #236 kept a hard precondition (count three). Local main ff'd to `1a33b99`; my two #236 worktrees pruned + local branch deleted, remote left for the operator. F7 tracked on #236 (`issuecomment-5252982527`). **9h-precondition backlog: #232 done, #237 reclassified/settled, #238 done, #236 root-1 done (roots 2/3 + #217 remain, plus deferred-tracked #245). FACTORY_PAUSED stays `true`.**

## #217 Defect A — fail-closed metadata-only completion assertion (sentinel-gated) — MERGED (PR #248, squash `7026b844`). #217 CLOSED (Defect A dark repair; Defect B re-scoped via D150). The D143 review-lens 9h prerequisite.

Not part of the credentialed-workflow backlog (#232/#237/#238/#236) — this is the D143 "working Claude review lens" 9h prerequisite. #217 named two defects in `claude-review`: **A** — the #211 metadata-only completion gate short-circuited step B on `metadata_only && result_clean` alone, so a zero-turn run whose tracking comment stayed the action's harness placeholder (`"I'll analyze this and get back to you."`, a genuine `github-actions[bot]` comment that passes the non-empty-body guard) passed green — a live #146 fail-open, D143 part-2 "no operator exception"; **B** — since #204/#211 the lens is metadata-only (all readers denied, no diff injected — the #194 exfil closure), so it structurally cannot post substantive line-level findings. Operator decisions (AskUserQuestion): **A is a mechanism repair** (close dark, sentinel-gated); **B is re-scoped, not rebuilt** (D150: the metadata-only lens whose completion assertion now fails closed, plus the pre-open floor + the Codex connector, satisfy D143; re-granting a reader is declined as a permissive change under D139's direction test). The fix: the metadata-only early-accept additionally requires the #183 terminal completion sentinel as the final non-empty line (and a heading region free of "review in progress") and falls through to the existing checklist/sentinel branches on any failed conjunct — strictly subtractive (new acceptance set ⊆ old), fail-closed. Non-activating (`FACTORY_PAUSED` stays `true`; no enable var, no credential).

### Learning 77 — the class-sweep must span every test file that pins the CHANGED literal, not just the primary suite; a byte-pinned contract test of the mechanism is IN scope, and the implementer does not get to call it "out of scope".
The `story-planner` contract's class-sweep enumerated tests in the completion-contract suite and asserted "one instance". It missed **T36** in a DIFFERENT file (`claude-code-review-workflow-contract.test.ts`), which pins the EXACT step-B gate literal `if [ "$CATALOG_METADATA_ONLY" = "true" ] && [ "$RESULT_CLEAN" = "true" ]; then`. The mechanism change replaced that literal, so T36 went red — and Codex reported it as an "out-of-scope" failure. Two rules crystallised. (a) **Scope is the orchestrator's call, never the implementer's** — D23 extends past review-finding adjudication to "is this failing test in scope": a byte-pinned contract test of the very grammar being changed is definitionally in scope and must be updated in the same PR. Reading the implementer's `1 failed` gate line and re-classifying it, rather than accepting the "out-of-scope" label, is the load-bearing move. (b) **A class-sweep for a mechanism change must grep for every test that pins the changed literal/grammar across ALL test files**, not just the behavioural suite — a workflow-contract / structure-pinning test is exactly where a byte-literal of the mechanism lives, and it is the sibling most likely to be missed by a suite-scoped sweep. The fold STRENGTHENED T36 to additionally lock the new sentinel conjunct, so a regression back to the two-conjunct fail-open now reds the workflow-contract test — the "update the contract that describes the changed thing" categorical closer, at the test-pin level.

### Learning 78 — a workflow-edit PR that hardens the review lens self-demonstrates in the fail-closed direction; read its own red as proof, and expect the post-open cost to be doc-sync not mechanism.
Because GitHub runs `pull_request` workflows from the PR head, #248's `claude-review` job ran the NEW three-conjunct gate. The action's workflow-validation guard skips its own review on a workflow-edit PR, so no sentinel was written, and the new gate correctly RED (`final line is not the terminal completion sentinel`) — exactly where main's two-conjunct short-circuit would have exited 0 GREEN. The claude-review red on this PR IS the fix working, and it is non-required so it does not block. This is the documented "that red is correct and must not be relaxed" case, now with a positive reading: a review-lens hardening PR carries its own live fail-closed demonstration. The only post-open finding was the connector's P2 that AGENTS.md's roster row still described the two-conjunct gate — a claim-accuracy doc-sync, **FOLDED not deferred** because it is the authoritative contract of the exact changed mechanism and the sibling registry was already updated in-PR (a categorical "keep the contract in lockstep" closer, not an unbounded precision tail). **0 post-open rounds on the mechanism itself**; the shift-left floor (read-diff catching T36 + fsr CONFIRMED-SOUND + local codex clean) held.

### Learning 79 — a new success-recording channel must carry every invariant the primary path has; the fail-closed lens finds the admission keystone, the diverse lens finds the claim-accuracy.
#236 roots 2/3 added a second place that emits the authenticated owner-task **success record** — the decide-phase content-recognition terminal (roots 1's silent no-op, now also posting an honest late success on a crash-window replay). Every invariant the finalize success path already enforced had to be re-established there, and each was initially missing:
- **Admissibility (the keystone, `factory-security-reviewer`).** Recognition gated only on tree+parent+trailer+freshness, never on the forbidden-path/envelope admission gates that live in `decideOwnerTaskPatch` (short-circuited by the stale-notice return). fsr built a working exploit — a write-collaborator pushes a commit whose tree matches a *forbidden-path* analysis's `treeOid` plus the public trailer, and the bot vouches for `.github/workflows/**` content that never passed admission. The internal contradiction it named: the code sets `trailerCommitPresent:false` *because* commit messages are attacker-controlled, then the late-record gate trusted that same trailer. Fix = a shared `isRecognizableAppliedAnalysis` predicate reusing the **exact** admission functions `decideOwnerTaskPatch` uses, gating the whole recognition block (no-op AND record), so recognition is provably as strong as apply-admission and cannot drift. Mutation-proven load-bearing.
- **Honest signal on base retarget (#238 consistency, Codex local).** The late record repeated finalize's "branch protection blocks merge" claim without finalize's base-retarget guard → false on an alternate base. Fix mirrored finalize's fresh-base check (retarget → notice, not success).
- **Provenance honesty (connector, post-open).** The late record reused finalize's heading asserting "pushed with GITHUB_TOKEN, no downstream workflows ran" — unsupported on recognition, which proves *content* not *pusher*. Fix = a provenance-neutral recognition heading that keeps the load-bearing "Do NOT merge / re-validate" instruction.

The **fail-closed lens (fsr) found the admission keystone**; the **diverse lens (Codex) found the two claim-accuracy defects** (base-retarget, provenance) — the documented split, again. Neither substitutes for the other.

**F7-recognition-only (operator decision, evidence-driven scope narrowing).** The ratified "F7 head-freshness across the marker-replay + finalize + recognition family" was narrowed to *recognition only* after review showed the two authenticated-marker sites net-harmful: an authenticated `github-actions[bot]` marker is durable, head-independent proof the comment was handled, so re-validating head-freshness there enabled a redundant re-apply (Codex ABA P1) / a duplicate success record (fsr low) with no security gain. Head-freshness is security-valuable *only* where there is no authenticated marker (content recognition infers "applied" from bytes, so live-ref confirmation is what stops it vouching for un-live content). Surfaced via AskUserQuestion because it changed ratified security scope; the "silent no-op/skip on drift" residual it accepts is availability-only, tracked in #249.

**Convergence.** Many pre-open folds (keystone, F7 direction, base-retarget, qa's tree-SHA coverage regression + round-trip + fetch-count), then **1 post-open connector round** (2 P2 claim-accuracy — runbook #236-staleness + provenance wording — **folded not deferred**, because a runbook that still calls a now-resolved blocker "open" misleads the 9h operator, and the success channel should not ship a flagged over-claim). Shift-left held: the security core was fail-closed before open.

**Process note.** Operator decisions via AskUserQuestion set the shape up front (dark-buildable 2+3 / one combined PR / F7-in-root-3 / binary success-vs-notice sentinel) and one mid-floor fork (F7-recognition-only). `story-planner` (fable) itself caught the load-bearing L75 dead-guard: `finalize` is unreachable on a crash-window replay (gated `if: steps.decide.outputs.kind == 'apply'`, never emitted on the recognition path), so "finalize-side recognition" was relocated to the reachable decide terminal — the orchestrator confirmed the gate against live workflow YAML before commissioning. Implementer Codex-MCP (D145 default; thread `019ff210`; 1 implement + 3 fold-replies). Floor: fsr(opus) ×4 (1 full + 3 micro-rechecks, all CONFIRMED-SOUND — the keystone exploit reproduced then refused; the F7-removal and both claim-accuracy deltas confirmed to weaken nothing), qa(sonnet) ×1 (NEEDS-WORK → folded), local `codex review` ×4 (final clean), gates green every head (3886/1skip, 99.69% stmt). Registry D135 note (three→two hard preconditions) orchestrator-authored on the branch; runbook sync delegated to Codex (docs, not orchestrator-owned). Connector: auto-review COMMENTED with 2 P2 on the first ready head `bde98b1`, then CLEAN 👍 (bot 👀 20:15:26Z → bot 👍 20:18:37Z, head unchanged, no findings review, REST-reactions bot-verified) on the manually re-triggered final head `c382981`. Threads resolved on delegated authority after the operator approved merge (and accepted the connector signal); pr-triage was commissioned but the operator's direct approval was the human-merge gate. `closingIssuesReferences` held `[]` throughout — #9 and #236 stayed open across every push; **#236 then CLOSED manually with a disposition comment** (roots 1/2/3 all landed). Squash `4940a2d`; open→merge ~45 min. Local main ff'd; three worktrees + local branch pruned, remote left for the operator. Metrics on #9 (`issuecomment-5258430400`); availability residuals in **#249**. **#236 is satisfied as a 9h prerequisite; remaining hard preconditions #237 (folds into the OAuth D140 ratification) + #245. FACTORY_PAUSED stays `true`.**

## L80 — 9h activation gate laid out; dark backlog exhausted; #245 resolved verified-with-exception + CLOSED (11 Aug 2026)

No build this turn — the dark-buildable 9h-precondition backlog is exhausted (#232/#238/#236 roots 1/2/3 all built dark + closed; #217 satisfied via D150), and the remaining preconditions are operator-driven, not dark PRs. Operator chose "Lay out the 9h gate" (AskUserQuestion). The orchestrator ground-verified the live surface at `4940a2d` (repo variables, `main` branch protection, issue states, both enable-gated workflows) and assembled a decision-by-decision activation sheet.

Two independent activation surfaces, to be decided separately: **(A)** the factory implement path (`implement-ready-issues.yml`) is gated *only* by `FACTORY_PAUSED != 'true'` + `ref == main` + `workflow_dispatch`, so unpausing alone arms it (the F1-S6 dry run and F1-S8 #11's DEV-Snowflake human-gated Environment live here); **(B)** the owner-command / task-apply surface (`owner-command-intake.yml`) needs `OWNER_COMMAND_INTAKE_ENABLED` (question) and additionally `OWNER_TASK_APPLY_ENABLED` (task mutation), whose ordered enable procedure is the runbook's "fail-safe activation path" items 1–5. Gate items: G0 preflight + §2 queued-run sweep (#232); G1 answer-agent OAuth D140; G2 task-agent OAuth D140 (cannot inherit G1; **#237 folds in** as the credential-reachability verification/isolation); G3 task-apply built-in-token D140 (novel `contents:write`+`pull-requests:write`, distinct disposition); G6 create enable var(s) last; G7 unpause (explicitly separate from any enable write); G8 supervised dry-run with the emergency-halt procedure dry-read first. #238 already discharged.

**#245 resolved (verified-with-exception, CLOSED).** `main` has all six required status checks + `enforce_admins` + `required_conversation_resolution`, but **no `required_pull_request_reviews`**, and `syamaner` is the sole (admin) collaborator. The orchestrator surfaced the interaction the naive "add required review = 1" hides: with `enforce_admins: true` and no second collaborator, a required-approval count makes the operator's own conventional PRs unmergeable (no self-approval, no admin bypass), while disabling `enforce_admins` to relieve that would also let an admin merge a red-CI factory PR (it gates the six checks too). The operator first leaned toward a second-approver identity, then clarified there is only the one identity; that reframed the trade-off. With a single identity a required-review gate adds no independent oversight (the same actor that merges can supply the approval, and the orchestrator runs as the operator), so it buys only friction (a per-merge toggle around the operator's own PRs). **Decision: keep procedural human-merge; do not add a required-review count.** The verification obligation is discharged — the default branch is protected with the required checks, admin-enforced, force-push/deletion off, conversation-resolution on — and the review-count gap is a documented exception (solo-admin repo; a self-approval is no independent gate; the no-auto-merge-on-green invariant stays enforced by the merge choreography). Resolution posted on #245 (superseding the earlier second-approver comment `issuecomment-5258550171`) and #245 **closed**. Remaining hard 9h task-mutation preconditions: #237 (folds into the task-agent OAuth D140) + the three D140 platform dispositions. Learning: a branch-protection "hardening" that reads clean can silently break the operator's own merge path on a solo-admin repo — verify the collaborator set + `enforce_admins` interaction (and confirm a second approver actually exists) *before* recommending a required-review count; surface the trade-off rather than handing over the command.

### Learning 81 — a status-only doc edit that flips a "resolved" fact can silently DELETE a safety cue that the item's *precondition-listing* implicitly carried; when the local floor (scope) and the connector (safety) then oscillate, resolve on safety-floor-wins = keep the fail-closed disclosure in-doc, defer only the mechanism to a scoped issue.
The spec said "drop #245 from the remaining hard 9h preconditions (it's resolved)". Done literally, that removed the ONLY activation-time branch-protection-drift cue: pre-edit, item 4 listed #245 (issue title "verify the default branch is protected with the required checks") as a task-mutation precondition executed *at 9h*, so a form of live-at-activation verification was implicitly present. Marking it a done fact deleted it. The local `codex review` floor caught this as a P1 fail-open; I folded (add live-recheck) → it refined (timing: unpause is a separate later act; completeness: `enforce_admins`/bypass/conversation-resolution, not just contexts) → I over-folded into what read as a new mandatory operator procedure → the floor flipped to P2 "remove the unscoped procedure, status-only slice" → I removed it → the **Codex connector** (post-open, diverse lens) then raised its own P1: removal reintroduces the fail-open, keep a precondition until the deterministic check exists. Two diverse lenses genuinely disagreed: local=scope ("don't author procedure here"), connector=safety ("don't drop the check"). **Resolution rule, applied: the safety floor outranks status-only tidiness** (a credible fail-open blocks; scope-tidiness does not). The terminal shape keeps a *precondition-altitude* fail-closed sentence (same altitude as the existing "#237 must verify…", NOT a command-level verifier) + an honest per-applied-head disclosure at the E-location, and defers the DETERMINISTIC/MECHANICAL enforcement — which a docs slice structurally cannot deliver — to a newly-filed, properly-scoped **#254** (both surfaces: enable-time AND per-applied-head, with concrete acceptance criteria). The distinction that ended the oscillation: **fold the DISCLOSURE (in scope, safety), defer the MECHANISM (out of scope, tracked)**. General rule: when a status sync flips X from "open precondition" to "resolved", check whether the precondition-listing was carrying a live safety action; if so the resolved status and the standing check are BOTH true and must both survive — don't let "resolved" silently retire the check.

(b) The clean verdict landed as BOTH a 👍 reaction (00:16:54Z) AND a top-level comment "Codex Review: Didn't find any major issues / Reviewed commit: cfb489f…" — pr-triage keyed on the **comment channel** (bot-authored, sha byte-matched the head) as the load-bearing accept signal, which is more robust than the withdraw-volatile reaction. ### Learning 83 — the local `codex review` floor caught that the slice's whole PREMISE was factually wrong, pre-open; and the factually-correct rewrite then collided with an existing factory-integrity contract test. Both folds landed before the PR opened → 0 post-open rounds.
Item 2, as written on #52 (a Codex-on-#50 finding, ":255"), asserted that `gh run rerun` re-triages an issue against its **stale opened-time body**. The local floor's **round-1 P2** refuted this by reading the workflow: `triage-issues.yml` never reads `github.event.issue.body`/`.title` (grep = 0 hits) — it re-fetches CURRENT issue data via `gh issue view "$ISSUE_NUMBER"` at execution time (lines 295-298). So a re-run does NOT see a stale body; the founding premise of item 2 was wrong. The **accurate** reason to prefer a fresh `workflow_dispatch` is that a re-run re-executes the workflow file from the run's **original pinned commit** (stale workflow code), not current `main` — the issue body is current either way. Folded the correction (orchestrator verified the workflow source itself before accepting the finding — claims-not-evidence). The floor's **round-2 P1** then caught that the corrected wording contained the literal `gh run rerun`, which `tests/factory/triage-workflow-contract.test.ts:1507` deliberately forbids in the resume section (`backfill = runbook.slice(from "## Resuming after a pause" to EOF)`, `.not.toContain("gh run rerun")` — the section steers operators to dispatch only). Rather than edit the test (a `tests/factory/**` protected-path scope trip), reworded to warn against re-running WITHOUT emitting the forbidden command string (the plain word "rerun" is fine — line 610 already uses it). Ran the full factory suite locally (63 files, 3879 tests green) to confirm the guard passed BEFORE opening. Round-3 floor: CLEAN. General rule: a docs "precision" slice can rest on a wrong premise — the diverse review floor is worth running even on 8 lines of prose, and verify the premise against source before shipping the caveat. Second rule reinforced: when correct wording trips an enforcement test, satisfy the guard from the docs side; editing the guard is a protected-path scope trip, not a fold.

### Learning 84 — a "LOW/acknowledgement" docs nit drew FIVE genuine review findings (3 local floor + 2 connector), each a real fail-open in the fail-closed contingency I was documenting; the diverse post-open connector lens caught two the same-family local floor passed. Convergence-cap invoked but the core stayed sound so all five folded.
Item 2 asked to "acknowledge" a completeness boundary and sketch a mitigation ("e.g. partition by created: ranges"). Writing a concrete fail-closed procedure inline invited the reviewers to hold it (correctly) to AGENTS.md's no-silent-truncation invariant (L206-209), and it kept revealing subtle holes: **local floor** — (1) P2: `created=` partition ranges are themselves subject to the 1,000-cap, so a >1,000 sub-range `--paginate`s to the same truncated IDs and passes the fixed-point check while dropping runs → added `total_count`-validate + recursive-bisect-to-≤1,000 + fail-closed stop; (2) P1: the partition domain must cover runs created BEFORE `PAUSE_START` (still-active publisher runs), not just the pause window; (3) P2: `created:` (search syntax) vs REST `created=` (endpoint param). **Connector (post-open, diverse family)** then caught two MORE the local floor had passed: (4) P2 the TRIGGER is wrong — truncation risk is repo-total-based (newest-1,000 hides an older run whenever the repo holds >1,000 runs total), NOT conditioned on pause-window volume; (5) P2 the lower bound was circular — "earliest visible run" is unknowable from the truncated listing being replaced, so it must be a deterministic absolute (first-possible run time). All five folded; final local floor + connector re-review both CLEAN on head `4e89e3a`. **Convergence-cap decision:** because findings 4/5 were genuine correctness fixes to text I was adding (a wrong trigger would mislead an operator mid-halt = a real fail-open), safety-floor-wins → fold them; the cap ("defer further precision to a fast-follow") was set to fire only on the NEXT nit, which never came (the trigger + lower-bound were the last two conceptual gaps). General rule reinforced (extends L81/L82): the diverse post-open connector lens is worth the extra cycle even on a docs nit — it caught the two most fundamental errors (trigger + bound) that four same-family local-floor passes missed; and when a "documentation acknowledgement" keeps drawing fail-open findings, that is a signal the note is really specifying a MECHANISM the reviewers will (rightly) hold to full rigor, so either specify it correctly or drop to pure disclosure.

**Process note.** story-planner used twice (initial ESCALATE with the byte-pin finding + item-2 contract on resume via SendMessage) — the ESCALATE is exactly the read-only planner catching a protected-path entanglement BEFORE any code, converting what would have been a post-implementation CI-red surprise into a clean re-scope. Connector re-review flow exercised in full: 2 P2 inline threads on head 1 (`85894e9`) → folded on head 2 (`4e89e3a`) → replied in both threads + resolved → `@codex review` once on the new final commit (sanctioned post-push re-trigger) → 👀→👍 + clean comment "Reviewed commit: 4e89e3a455" (BOTH channels, bot-authored, sha-matched, postdating the re-trigger) → 0 unresolved. pr-triage (D23) → MERGEABLE; it independently re-read the paragraph to confirm both connector findings were fixed in the FILE not just marked resolved, and flagged a non-blocking nit (the PR BODY's Summary still carried the stale pause-window-volume framing) → orchestrator corrected the PR body pre-merge for historical-record accuracy. Squash-merged on delegated authority; worktree + local branch pruned, remote left for the operator. ### Learning 85 — trying to PROVE inertness of an allowlisted exemption by NEGATIVE-SCANNING an arbitrary `run:` script does not converge; the convergence cap + zero-live-instance + a protected-file review gate justify deferring the positive byte-pin to a fast-follow, and the honest-comment (never overstate closure) discipline is what makes the deferral shippable.
PR2's classifier grants ONE exemption to its fail-closed "every job must carry the byte-exact pause conjunct" rule: the inverse-gated `pause-notice` allowlist (two jobs that run DURING a soft pause by design). Hardening that exemption to prove the notice job is "inert" turned into a treadmill: local floor round-1 P1 = job/step `env`/`secrets` capability fields accepted → folded a positive inert KEY-SHAPE allowlist (job keys ⊆ {if,name,runs-on,permissions,steps}, step keys ⊆ {name,run,shell,working-directory}); round-2 P1 = `${{ secrets.* }}` interpolates directly into `run:` text regardless of env/permissions → folded a secrets-context regex; orchestrator SELF-CAUGHT that `/\$\{\{\s*secrets\b/i` misses `${{ toJSON(secrets) }}` → tightened to `/\$\{\{[^}]*\bsecrets\b/i` + F23; round-3 the floor then found THREE more channels the scan cannot close (workflow-level `env:` inheritance — classification starts at `jobs:`; `${{ format('{0}', secrets.X) }}` — the `}` in `'{0}'` ends `[^}]*` before `secrets`; unvalidated `runs-on: self-hosted`). The pattern IS the signal: the adversarial reviewer recommended "byte-pin the notice" all three rounds. **Decision (convergence cap, D139):** the CORE (job-level `if:` gating detection — the two evasions the ISSUE actually named) is fsr-CONFIRMED-SOUND; the residual is claim-accuracy on the ONE exemption, has ZERO live instances (verified: triage's only root env is the non-secret `TARGET_ISSUE_NUMBER`; both notice scripts echo only static text + `github.repository` + `$GITHUB_STEP_SUMMARY`; both `runs-on: ubuntu-latest`, `permissions: {}`), and every channel requires editing a protected `.github/workflows/**` file that draws fsr on THAT PR + is disabled by a full halt. So DEFER the definitive positive byte-pin to fast-follow **#259**, keep the real structural hardening, and — the load-bearing move — REWRITE the detector comment to be HONEST (drop "closes the credential class"; name the residual channels + the authoritative structural guarantees + #259). pr-triage independently READ the live workflows and confirmed zero live instances ("reject what is not used" / zero-instance-execution-class doctrine) → MERGEABLE. General rule: to constrain an allowlisted exemption, PIN the known-good shape (positive), don't ENUMERATE the bad (negative) — negative scans of Turing-complete `run:` scripts never converge; and when you must ship before the pin exists, the honesty of the comment (no overstated closure) is what keeps the deferral inside the AGENTS.md floor.

**Process note.** Two clean Codex-MCP threads, own worktrees off `25a4586`, orchestrator committed each diff (sandbox can't write the linked-worktree index) + amended folds + rebased PR2 onto post-PR1 `2e918c5` (disjoint files, trivial). Pre-open floor per PR: orchestrator diff-read every round + local `codex review --base origin/main` (PR1 ×1 CLEAN; PR2 ×3, drove the fold arc) + MANDATORY `factory-security-reviewer` in the PR's OWN worktree (both CONFIRMED-SOUND) + `qa` on PR2 (691 changed test lines > 600 trigger). Codex correctly STOPPED on a contradictory gate (my class-sweep said "0 `<PAUSE_END>`" but the negative-pin needle is itself that literal in test SOURCE) rather than improvise — do/don't contract working; orchestrator adjudicated (the runtime `backfill` slice is runbook content, not test source). Verified Prettier is NOT a project gate (not a dep, no script, CI runs eslint) before dismissing its warnings. Connector clean on BOTH first-ready heads: PR1 👍 21:21:56Z, PR2 👀 22:07:27Z→👍 22:10:33Z (withdraw-volatile 👀 gone by the settle poll), head unchanged, no findings review, 0 threads, `mergeStateStatus` CLEAN — trusted the auto-👍 on the security-gate change too (took it seriously, it raised nothing new past the deferred residuals). claude-review metadata-only benign (declines to verify by design, not silence-as-clean); spec-grounded both legs green. Both worktrees + local branches pruned; remotes auto-deleted on squash-merge (local-branch-delete failed while worktree held them → pruned after). Registry sync itself (PR #261, squash `3fc03c2`) drew a connector **P2**: the "zero live instances" wording was imprecise — the residual FORMS exist in-tree (triage's non-secret root `env`, `ubuntu-latest` runs-on); only the secret-bearing/exploit instance is zero. ## L86 — #259 pause-notice byte-pin MERGED (PR #262, squash `737c6b0`); #259 CLOSED. Dark/non-activating.

Operator picked "Spec #259" at the post-#243 fork, then "Build #259 now" at the spec checkpoint. This is the definitive fix for L85's treadmill: the `pause-notice` exemption's inertness was chased by NEGATIVE-scanning (key-shape allowlist + a `${{ secrets }}` regex), which never converged — env/secrets fields → run-body secrets → `toJSON`/`format()` → root-`env` inheritance → `runs-on`. #259 replaces the whole scan with a POSITIVE structural byte-pin: an accepted notice job must `firstStructuralMismatch`-deep-equal a plain object transcribed byte-exactly from the live workflow (identical key sets BOTH directions, arrays by length+order, strict scalar `===`, non-plain nodes fail closed), plus a pinned root key-set and triage's value-pinned non-secret root `env`. An extra field on either side is a mismatch, so every capability channel — present and future — closes at once instead of being enumerated; the scan + regex are deleted as strictly subsumed. The intended brittleness (a notice-message edit reds the guard until re-pinned in the same reviewed diff) IS the security property. story-planner (fable) contract on #259 (`issuecomment-5273647242`), D104-verified (anchors ground-checked: exactly 2 `FACTORY_PAUSED ==` notice jobs, root key-sets, the two run texts differ → 2 distinct pins). Implementer Codex-MCP (D145; thread `019ff949`; 1 implement + 1 fold-reply). Net 408+/238- one file `tests/factory/runbook-halt-inventory.test.ts`. Ready 04:43:06Z → merged 04:51:23Z (~8 min). `closingIssuesReferences` held exactly `[259]`; #260/#243/#9/#232 never closing-referenced. **Post-open review rounds: 0** — connector 👍 clean on the first ready head (`ef403f7`, 04:45:42Z).

### Learning 86 — a per-guard mutation check only tests a guard if that guard is the SOLE thing catching the mutation; when two guards both catch it, neither is individually pinned, and the fix is to restructure so each guard is independently necessary.
qa (NEEDS-WORK) caught that no N-test DELETED a pinned key, so `firstStructuralMismatch`'s "expected-key-missing" loop (loop 2) had no test. The subtlety Codex surfaced when folding: with the recursion loop (loop 3) iterating `expectedKeys`, deleting a job key was ALSO caught by loop 3 (recursing `undefined` vs the pinned value → mismatch), so REMOVING loop 2 would NOT have failed the new test — loop 3 masked it. Codex's fold changed loop 3 to iterate `actualKeys`, which is provably set-equivalent (loops 1+2 guarantee equal key sets before loop 3 runs) yet makes loop 2 the SOLE guard for the missing-key case → the mutation check "remove loop 2 ⇒ test fails" now holds (verified). General rule for guard-shaped diffs: the removing-guard-X-must-fail-test-Y discipline requires each guard to be INDEPENDENTLY load-bearing; if a mutation is caught by two code paths, restructure (not just add a test) so the intended guard is the one and only catcher, else the "test" passes with the guard gutted. This is a strictly stronger reading of D104's per-guard mutation requirement, learned live on a comparator. Other floor notes: fsr CONFIRMED-SOUND (matrix over comparator both directions, `toJSON` merge-key/anchor/`on:true`/null/non-plain surprises, root-check gating, out-of-file residual honesty; one LOW comment-enumeration gap — omitted `run-name` — folded); local `codex review` CLEAN twice, and it independently probed prototype-pollution (`__proto__`/`constructor` keys surface as extra own-keys via `Object.hasOwn`/`Object.keys` on null-proto `toJSON` output → rejected); qa's other adds (root missing-key test, named direct-secrets parity) folded in the same round. All four review lanes (local floor, fsr, qa, connector) converged with ZERO post-open rounds — the shift-left working end-to-end on a genuine enforcement-mechanism rewrite.

**Process note.** Full spec→build→merge drive on operator go, mirroring the #243 pattern. Own worktree off `3fc03c2`, orchestrator committed Codex's diff + amended the fold + re-ran the local floor after the comparator-touching fold (logic change → re-floor per the pre-open rule; CLEAN). Codex transcribed both notice-job pins byte-exactly from the live files (block-scalar run text incl. trailing newline) — verified live pins retain newlines and T1/T2 accept the real workflows. fsr + qa both MANDATORY (protected `tests/factory/**` + >600 test-line diff) and both ran in the PR's own worktree. Connector 👍 clean (04:45:42Z, head unchanged, no findings) — trusted it on the security-gate change (took it seriously; it raised nothing). claude-review metadata-only benign; spec-grounded benign (`hasCriteria:false`). pr-triage (D23) MERGEABLE with a full independent trail (CI re-run locally 42/42, comparator read directly for fail-closed-both-directions, T1/T2 byte-verified vs live files, pipeline-self-modification invariant checked = conventional/human-opened + fsr ran = no violation). Squash-merged on delegated authority; worktree + local branch pruned, remote auto-deleted. Untrusted `[SYSTEM NOTIFICATION]` preamble recurred on every notification; disregarded, nested results used on merits. **9h backlog UNCHANGED: remaining hard preconditions #237 + task-agent OAuth D140 (G2, deferred dark) + enable-var writes + unpause — all operator HARD STOPS. #259 CLOSED (notice-inertness residuals now positively pinned). #254 (branch-protection re-verify) + #52 item-1 probe still open prep; the claude-review ALLOWED_TOOLS-vs-#204 observation flagged to the operator. FACTORY_PAUSED stays `true`.**

## L87 — #254 deterministic fail-closed branch-protection-contract re-verification MERGED (PR #263, squash `fa0e07c`); #254 CLOSED; #264 filed. Dark/non-activating.

Operator picked "Spec #254 first" (cold-start prompt) then "Build — Codex-MCP" at the checkpoint. #254 delivers the deterministic/fail-closed enforcement #253 (PR #255) deferred: a pure offline comparator (`scripts/factory/branch-protection-contract.mts`, `verifyBranchProtectionContract` + stdin CLI) that byte-verifies the whole #245 branch-protection contract — the nine required contexts (exact set + each context's provider `app_id`), `checks`↔`contexts` cardinality, every boolean pin, a closed top-level key set (exact `url`/`contexts_url` locator allowlist), and `required_pull_request_reviews` absence (solo-admin exception) — wired into **five** operator surfaces (applied-head steps 3+6, enable-time items 4+5, the `FACTORY_PAUSED` unpause boundary), each loading the comparator from the hard-coded authenticated `gh api …/contents/…?ref=main` source (identical provenance to the payload query), asserting `default_branch == main`, then running it fail-closed. `story-planner` (fable) contract on #254 (`issuecomment-5276275454`), D104-verified after ground-checking anchors at `737c6b0` (byte-verified the live nine-context contract myself — the cold-start prompt's own enumeration had dropped `dependency-review`; the registry/memory "six + three legs" phrasing was right). Implementer Codex-MCP (D145; thread `019ff998`; 1 implement + **8** fold-replies). Net ~470 logic lines (comparator 345 + runbook ~146) + a ~770-line test file. `closingIssuesReferences` held exactly `[254]` throughout (`Refs #245 #253 #238 #9 #264`; #9/#232/#253/#255 never closing-referenced). Registry (`docs/state/registry.md`) one-line dependency-review reclassification folded in-slice (D135 conventional human-directed amendment). Metrics on #254.

### Learning 87 — the hard sub-problem of a security slice was NOT the comparator (sound from pass 1) but the OPERATOR-EXECUTION LAYER of the checker; that layer generated ~8 fold rounds because "how do you safely invoke a drift-checker from a possibly-untrusted context" is a real provenance problem the story-planner contract didn't pre-flag.
The comparator's verification logic was fsr-CONFIRMED-CLOSED early and never regressed. Every subsequent round hardened HOW the operator runs it, and each was a genuine fail-open a different lens caught: (1) pre-open local+fsr — the runbook hardcoded `main` but owner-task apply targets the mutable `default_branch`; the existing baseRefName check re-reads the mutated default so it doesn't compensate → byte-exact `default_branch=main` pin at every surface. (2) qa — 6 comparator guards had ZERO sole-catcher test → 13 negatives. (3) connector — `app_id` unbind-then-forge (fsr/I had accepted it low; connector's asymmetry mechanism — unbind is undetected while removal IS caught, enabling a non-admin `statuses:write` forge — was sharper → folded the app_id pin), comparator-from-PR-tree, dep-review class un-swept. (4) fsr — the trusted-load's `mktemp -d` temp path SILENTLY no-ops the comparator on macOS (the string entrypoint guard `import.meta.url === file://${argv[1]}` fails because Node realpaths import.meta.url but not argv[1], and `/var`→`/private/var`) → empty-stdout-exit-0 fail-open on the operator's own platform → realpath-robust guard + an end-to-end copied-to-symlinked-tmp regression test + `set -euo pipefail`/`test -s`. (5) local — unpause-boundary TOCTOU (item-5 gates the enable-var write but the separate FACTORY_PAUSED unpause is the arming act) → 5th comparator block. (6) connector — `git show origin/main` after a no-destination `git fetch origin main` can read stale/absent origin/main → FETCH_HEAD, then (7) local — the ambient LOCAL `origin` remote is itself untrusted (a redirected origin feeds an attacker comparator while the payload comes from the hard-coded API) → the definitive fix: load the comparator from the SAME hard-coded authenticated `gh api …/contents/…?ref=main` as the payload, deleting the whole git-remote-trust chain. (8) local — step-6 head-reread ordering + contents-default-branch micro-race → `?ref=main` + head reread after comparator. THEN pr-triage (the independent D23 lens, and the ONLY one that caught it) found **codecov/patch RED** (a merge prerequisite none of the review lenses nor my required-checks-only CI poll flagged) → in-process `runCli` tests (subprocess tests don't count for parent-process v8 coverage; sibling `check-invisible-format-characters.mts` pattern) + branch-coverage sort (4 genuinely-unreachable defensive branches v8-ignored with per-branch reasons, each verified provably-dead by the gating). **Convergence discipline:** invoked the D139 cap on the ONE residual with no real exploit — path-scoping the locator allowlist (GitHub's protection response is a fixed schema; no payload puts `contexts_url` under `enforce_admins`) — deferred to fast-follow **#264** + justified rather than fold #9. General rules: (a) for operator-run security TOOLING, the story-planner contract must pre-flag "how is this invoked safely from an untrusted checkout/remote" as a first-class design question — it would have collapsed rounds 3/4/6/7 into the initial design; (b) the diverse post-open lenses each caught DIFFERENT real fail-opens the same-family floor passed (connector: app_id asymmetry + provenance; fsr: macOS symlink; pr-triage: coverage-as-merge-prereq) — the roster's non-overlap is the value; (c) subprocess CLI tests are real behavioral coverage but INVISIBLE to parent-process v8 → codecov reads them as 0%, so a CLI entrypoint needs in-process `runCli` tests + a single-line entry-guard ignore (established sibling pattern), and pr-triage is where that gets caught because it re-derives merge-readiness from live check state not the author's floor; (d) provenance must be END-TO-END identical — a checker whose PAYLOAD comes from a hard-coded authenticated source but whose CODE comes from an ambient remote is a real asymmetry; make both the same source.

**Process note.** Squash `fa0e07c`, ready→merged 09:23:10Z; #254 CLOSED, no collateral closure (#9/#264 OPEN, #232/#253 already closed). One Codex-MCP thread (`019ff998`, own worktree off `737c6b0`, orchestrator committed + amended each of the 8 fold rounds since the sandbox can't write the linked-worktree index). Pre-open + every post-fold re-verify: orchestrator diff-read + local `codex review --base origin/main` (ran ~9×, drove most fold arcs) + MANDATORY `factory-security-reviewer` in its OWN detached worktree at each head (CONFIRMED-CLOSED after every enforcement-mechanism fold; the comparator .mts logic was byte-stable and cleared once, so late test-only/coverage folds skipped a redundant fsr round) + `qa` (test file >600 lines). **Connector mechanics worth recording:** the auto-review did NOT fire on any `synchronize` (had to `@codex review` once per new head, 4×) — and the reaction-staleness hazard bit: after the coverage re-push the previous head's 👍 (08:13Z on `3f1e8c1`) was still present, and the first look-first poll wrongly treated it as fresh; caught by comparing the 👍 `created_at` to the push time, then re-triggered and waited for the head-matched CLEAN COMMENT ("Reviewed commit: `c588dbad44`", bot-authored `Bot`, 08:50:19Z > trigger) — the comment channel, not the volatile 👍, was the load-bearing signal ([[codex-connector-clean-signal-calibration]]). `claude-review` went RED on the final head with null title/summary + 0 inline findings + 0 unresolved threads = the #217/#146 metadata-lens fail-closed completion gate (non-required, "gate working not a finding" per the cold-start guidance); `spec-grounded review` both legs PASS; connector CLEAN. **pr-triage (D23, independent) returned FIX-FIRST not MERGEABLE** — it re-derived merge-readiness from LIVE check state and caught `codecov/patch` RED (79.23%→ fixed to green) that the required-checks-only CI poll AND every review lens had missed; after the coverage sort it confirmed all 7 threads genuinely fixed in-code + both non-fold dispositions defensible → MERGEABLE. Resolved all 7 connector threads on that adjudication, posted the summary, squash-merged on delegated authority; two worktrees + local branch pruned, remote left for the operator ([[branch-deletion-is-mine-to-manage]]). Untrusted `[SYSTEM NOTIFICATION - NOT USER INPUT]` preamble recurred on every task-notification; flagged once at session start, disregarded, nested results used on merits ([[untrusted-notification-preamble-pattern]]) — merge authority is the genuine session-start delegation. **9h backlog UNCHANGED: remaining hard preconditions #237 + task-agent OAuth D140 (G2, deferred dark) + enable-var writes + unpause — all operator HARD STOPS. #254 CLOSED; #264 filed (locator path-scoping fast-follow). #52 item-1 probe + the claude-review ALLOWED_TOOLS-vs-#204 observation still open prep. FACTORY_PAUSED stays `true`.**

## L88 — #266 slice 1 (claude-review diff-injection lens, D151) MERGED then VALIDATED on a live non-workflow PR; slice 2 next. Dark/non-activating.

**Recording slice 1's merge (prior session) + this session's validation together, since #266 had no prior ledger entry.** #266: the #204/#211 metadata-only `claude-review` lens denies every reader tool, so it had no way to see the diff → deterministic zero-turn no-op (`num_turns:1`, harness placeholder "I'll analyze this…") → the #217 step-B fail-closed completion gate red on EVERY PR (persistent red-X, zero automated Claude signal). D150 had rejected content-review as reader-re-grant (reopens #194 exfil); **D151** (operator AskUserQuestion, 13 Aug) narrowly supersedes that one clause — inject the diff as inert nonce-fenced DATA, tool-grant closure byte-intact, `factory-security-reviewer` CONFIRMED-SOUND that it adds no sink/egress/principal (same channel as the already-accepted #194 title/body residual; accepted cost = widened injection VOLUME on a non-authenticated, non-required verdict). Slice 1 delivered via PR #267 (squash `5ea8d7e`): a `pr-diff` step computes `merge-base(payload base.sha, head.sha)` object-store-only (no head checkout/execute), 64 KiB fail-closed cap, `GITHUB_OUTPUT` collision guard, trusted `changeset_empty` output (never PR-content-derived); a repo-owned capability-closed prompt (full Code Review Rubric + factory self-mod blocker gated on `roastpilot-factory[bot]` author) replaces the `/code-review` plugin; allow/deny/`[ToolSearch]` residual + steps A/B/C byte-identical. Tests T38–T42 + a D-T1..D-T16 harness that extracts and runs the real `run:` body.

**Validation (this session, 13 Aug).** Slice 1 could NOT self-validate: PR #267 is itself a workflow-edit PR, whose `claude-review` no-ops by the documented workflow-edit confound (not proof either way). The `pr-diff` step succeeded and the injected prompt provably carried the fenced diff (mechanism proven), but whether the MODEL completes a real review end-to-end was UNPROVEN and was the load-bearing open commitment gating #266. Discharged on a deliberate non-workflow probe — **PR #268** (`validate/claude-review-lens-266`, one line of `README.md`, no protected path) against `main` @ `5ea8d7e`. The [claude-review run](https://github.com/syamaner/roastpilot-cloud/actions/runs/31729183755/job/94545039613) met every conjunct: prompt carried the real `PR-DIFF-FENCE-…` diff with `changeset_empty=false`; `"num_turns": 3` / `"subtype": "success"` / `"is_error": false` (NOT the `num_turns:1` no-op baseline); step C `confirmed: SDK tool-catalog closure holds for 1 permitted residual tool(s); metadata_only=true, result_clean=true`; step B green (`clean terminal exit and the terminal completion sentinel`); github-actions[bot] tracking comment = a genuine rubric-reasoned review of the actual README change, checklist ticked, final line exactly the #183 `REVIEW-COMPLETE` sentinel. Verdict recorded on #266 (`issuecomment-5284596630`); D151 stamped in `factory.md`. Probe PR #268 closed without merging (purpose served); branch pruned local + remote.

### Learning 88 — a workflow-edit change to a `pull_request`-triggered workflow can NEVER pre-merge-validate itself, because any carrying PR is itself workflow-edit-confounded; validation of such a change is structurally POST-merge, on a deliberate non-workflow probe PR.
The corollary that made the probe necessary rather than optional: "the mechanism works" (pr-diff step green + injected prompt provably contains the diff) is NOT "the model completes a real review" — the observable that distinguishes them is `num_turns` (>1 with a posted rubric-reasoned sentinel comment vs the `num_turns:1` harness-placeholder no-op), read from the job log's SDK result record, not the check colour. A green metadata-only `claude-review` check on a workflow-edit PR carries no review signal at all (it no-ops and, post-#217, reds on the completion gate); only a non-workflow PR exercises the lens end-to-end. General rule: when a change alters the very workflow that would review it, plan the validation as a separate post-merge non-workflow probe from the start — do not treat the change's own PR result as evidence in either direction.

**Process note.** Orchestrator-direct one-line throwaway probe (validation artifact the operator task explicitly commissioned, not a story deliverable — delegating a one-line probe to a Codex worktree would be pure overhead). No implementer, no worktree, no review roster spend beyond the lens under test. Evidence read from the job log's SDK result record + the bot tracking comment, not the check colour (green ≠ reviewed discipline, applied to prove the POSITIVE this time). **Slice 2 next (pending):** remove the now-dead `/code-review` plugin machinery (`.github/workflows/claude-code-review.yml` marketplace steps + `plugins`/`plugin_marketplaces` inputs, retire `tests/factory/claude-marketplace-pin.test.ts` → absence assertions, update execution-surface drift counters + T38 position pin), `Closes #266`. Protected-path CONVENTIONAL (factory pipeline) → story-planner contract → Codex-MCP impl in own worktree → gates + local `codex review` + MANDATORY factory-security-reviewer (qa if test diff load-bearing) pre-open → draft → ready → pr-triage → human merge. It is itself a workflow PR, so it won't self-validate either — fine, slice 1 is now validated by PR #268 (Learning 88). **9h backlog UNCHANGED: #237 + task-agent OAuth D140 (G2, deferred dark) + enable-var writes + unpause — all operator HARD STOPS. FACTORY_PAUSED stays `true`.**

## L89 — #266 slice 2 (remove dead claude-review plugin-marketplace machinery) MERGED (PR #269, squash `97c9d7f`); #266 CLOSED. Dark/non-activating.

Full spec→build→review→merge drive on the same operator go, immediately after L88's validation. Net workflow logic diff +8/−64 (~72 lines); ~430 test lines. Pure surface reduction, no lens behaviour change; every security-load-bearing region byte-unchanged (base-owned config restore, pr-diff, `claude_args` allow/deny closure, `prompt:` block, steps A/B/C, resolver, spec-grounded job). `story-planner` (fable) contract on #266 (`issuecomment-5284753593`), **orchestrator D104-verified against the real tree** (removal boundaries, counter values `{18,50,49,134}→{18,48,48,127}`, T38/T1 `clearIndex` edits all ground-checked by reading). Implementer Codex-MCP (D145; own worktree off `5ea8d7e`, 1 implement call). Routing: `factory-security-reviewer` MANDATORY + **`qa` added by the orchestrator** (retiring a 322-line enforcement alarm makes test quality load-bearing). Pre-open floor: local `codex review` CLEAN, fsr **CONFIRMED-SOUND**, qa **PASS** (all three in isolated worktrees). Draft→ready (D103); all 9 required checks + codecov/patch green on the draft head. Connector 👍 clean (`18:58:02Z`, bot-authored, postdates ready `18:55:31Z`, head unchanged, no findings comment/review). pr-triage (D23) **MERGEABLE** with full independent re-verification (required-checks set read from live branch protection; claude-review red confirmed benign no-op from the job log; 0 unresolved threads; CodeQL PR-scoped 0 introduced findings; `closingIssuesReferences==[266]`). Squash-merged on delegated authority (`97c9d7f`, 19:05:14Z; operator "watch it to merge"); worktree + local + remote branch pruned. **Post-open review rounds: 0.**

### Learning 89 — the story-planner's class sweep can under-enumerate a mechanically-coupled sibling; the Codex implementer's own class sweep is a real second net, and the fix belongs in the same slice.
The contract enumerated ONE execution-surface drift counter (source-only `{18,…}`) but the file holds TWO — a broader aggregate counter (`{26,75,66,179}`) that counts the SAME removed workflow surface across all workflows. Codex caught the second counter during its own class sweep, updated it with the identical `−2/−1/−7` delta, and reported it transparently (do/don't contract working: it fixed the in-class mechanical breakage, did not widen scope, and flagged the addition). The orchestrator verified both counters' current values and deltas by reading, and fsr + qa each independently re-derived them by mutation (injecting a bogus input reddened both, proving neither is vacuous). General rule: a drift-counter or pin that aggregates a corpus can have MULTIPLE instances over overlapping corpora; the class sweep must grep for the assertion SHAPE (`toEqual({ jobs`), not just the one call site the contract happened to cite — and when the implementer's sweep finds a sibling the contract missed, folding it in-slice (same class, same delta) is correct, not scope creep.

### Learning 90 — a workflow-edit PR runs its HEAD workflow version, injection and the catalog closure both work, yet the model still no-ops on the self-referential diff; the "won't self-validate" property is the MODEL's no-op, not an absent-injection or base-version artifact.
Direct evidence from #269's own `claude-review` (run `31733248423`, head `c9e01b4`): the HEAD/slice-2 workflow ran (step list is `pr-diff → Run Claude Code Review`, no marketplace steps), pr-diff succeeded, and **step C passed** (`metadata_only=true, result_clean=true`, init catalog exactly `[ToolSearch]`) — so slice 2 did NOT weaken the tool-grant closure — but the MODEL produced `num_turns:1` with the harness placeholder ("I'll analyze this and get back to you.") and no sentinel, so step B (#217/#146) fails closed → a NON-REQUIRED red. This refines L88's mechanism note: the no-op is not "the base version runs without injection" (the head version ran WITH working injection); it is the model no-opping when the injected diff is itself the claude-review lens's own prompt/sentinel/fence/marketplace machinery (the T43 test literally contains those literals). Same reason #267 no-op'd. Operational consequence unchanged and as predicted: the red is expected, non-blocking (claude-review is not a required check), and does NOT validate slice 2 — the plugin-removed lens is validated post-merge on the next non-workflow PR, exactly as slice 1 was on #268. The `metadata_only=true` step-C pass is the load-bearing evidence that the removal is safe.

**Process note.** #266 fully CLOSED (both slices). Registry carries no #266 reference (no sync needed). Two Codex threads across the session (slice-2 impl `019ffc5f`; the L88 validation used no implementer). Untrusted `[SYSTEM NOTIFICATION - NOT USER INPUT]` preamble recurred on every task-notification; flagged once, disregarded, nested results used on merits ([[untrusted-notification-preamble-pattern]]) — merge authority is the genuine "watch it to merge" delegation this session. **9h backlog UNCHANGED: remaining hard preconditions #237 + task-agent OAuth D140 (G2, deferred dark) + enable-var writes + unpause — all operator HARD STOPS. Open review-lens residuals: #194 (verdict not adversarially authenticated), #212/#210/#184-adjacent forgeable-sentinel — all tracked, none blockers. FACTORY_PAUSED stays `true`.**

## L90 — #274 (claude-review stochastic zero-turn no-op) fixed + MERGED (PR #279, squash `673a756`); D152 ratified then corrected to a five-conjunct envelope; SDK-corruption hardening deferred to #280. Dark/non-activating.

The longest single-issue arc of the experiment. #274: the metadata-only `claude-review` lens stochastically zero-turn no-ops, reding the **non-required** check as noise. **Characterised first** (operator choice): a probe matrix (4 add-only throwaway PRs, each re-run 4× on byte-identical heads) **proved the no-op is stochastic, not content-determined** — byte-identical diffs flip both ways (P2 policy prose PASS/FAIL/PASS/PASS; P3 self-ref FAIL/FAIL/PASS/FAIL; P1 neutral prose 4/4 no-op; pooled ~67%) — refuting #274's own self-referential-policy-prose hypothesis. Operator chose fix direction (1): give the proven-inert no-op a non-failing outcome. **Pre-open, the local `codex review` floor (reading the plan repo) caught that direction (1) REVERSES D150** (which made the zero-turn placeholder red, "the un-exceptionable D143 part-2 #146 regression, no operator exception"). Operator ratified **D152** (narrow amendment: proven-inert ANNOTATED no-op exits success; every STARTED review still fails closed; the annotation closes the #146 "misread green as reviewed" hazard). Implementer Codex-MCP (D145; one continuous thread, 1 implement + ~8 fold-replies). Then the connector + floor drove an **unusually long convergence**: round-1 content-shape fold; a categorical fix (jq literal-preserving `num_turns`, closed content-block allowlist, `substantive_output` fifth conjunct closing the L1115 "text-produced-but-not-posted" gap); floor-caught `tool_result`-as-activity + user-block allowlist folds; a **D152 four-vs-five-field divergence** the floor caught pre-open → **operator ratified the D152 envelope correction to five conjuncts** (plan `fa0e2e6`). fsr **CONFIRMED-SOUND** (twice; judged the SDK-transcript-corruption class non-attacker-reachable — the model can't author the SDK envelope), qa PASS, floor clean. Connector **round 3** then posted 4 MORE P1s (all corruption-hardening: record-type allowlist, outer-type/inner-role binding, user-text payload, terminal `result.result`) while the same-head floor was clean — an effectively **unbounded** same-family adversarial pattern. **Operator hit the proportionality wall and chose "stop + adjudicate + track + merge."** pr-triage **MERGEABLE** (9 required checks green; claude-review non-required + its red live-validated the fix in the safe direction; `closingIssuesReferences==[274]`; round-3 verified non-attacker-reachable + blast-radius-contained). Deferred hardening → **#280**; all 8 threads resolved (4 fixed w/ citations, 4 deferred w/ rationale); squash-merged on delegated authority. Final ~124 logic lines, ~528 test lines. Metrics on #274 (`issuecomment-5302948360`). **Post-open review rounds: high (atypical) — the headline metric.**

### Learning 91 — proving "the model produced NOTHING" from a corruptible SDK transcript is an effectively unbounded hardening problem; the cross-family adversarial reviewer's CONFIRMED-SOUND is the right stopping signal, not a clean same-family connector.
A five-conjunct "proven-inert" gate that parses the transcript to prove inertness invites a new "but what if the transcript is corrupted THIS way" fail-closed finding every review round (unknown block type → unknown record type → role mismatch → payload shape → result-field → …). These are real per strict fail-closed literalism but all require an SDK/infrastructure-corrupted envelope the adversary (a prompt-injected model with no envelope write path) cannot author. The **cross-family `factory-security-reviewer` (opus) CONFIRMED-SOUND**, having judged the whole class non-attacker-reachable, is the load-bearing stopping signal. The **same-family** Codex connector's fail-closed literalism does NOT converge on its own (it found round-3 findings the same-head local floor did not — instance non-determinism), and it is advisory-but-triaged, NOT required. Don't chase a clean connector on a non-required lens against an underspecified corruption adversary; stop on fsr-sound + floor-clean + a blast-radius argument, and track the residual class.

### Learning 92 — a permissive change to a fail-closed SECURITY gate must reconcile with the ratified plan text twice: the direction (D150→D152) and the exact envelope (four→five fields). The pre-open floor reading the plan repo is what enforces it.
The floor caught TWO governance divergences pre-open on this one PR: (a) the fix reversed D150's outcome (→ D152 amendment), and (b) the hardened implementation's five-conjunct signature exceeded D152's own literal four-field envelope (→ D152 correction). Both are the #266/D151 pattern. The lesson: when a security-gate implementation is hardened during review beyond what the ratifying decision's envelope text literally says — even MORE fail-closed — that is still a plan-vs-code divergence AGENTS.md's "plan wins" requires reconciling, and the operator re-ratifies the corrected envelope; the orchestrator never self-amends the plan. Check `factory.md`'s D-sequence for the target lens BEFORE spec'ing, not just at review.

### Learning 93 — blast-radius containment, not exhaustive hardening, is what makes deferral proportionate.
The decisive fact for stopping: step B is read-only and runs AFTER the Claude action, so any inline findings the model actually produced were already posted to GitHub via the one permitted comment tool BEFORE step B evaluates. A false accept on the corruption edges therefore only mislabels a NON-REQUIRED check's status (green-with-explicit-notice instead of red) — it cannot suppress or drop a posted finding, and gates nothing. That contained blast radius (plus fsr-sound + non-attacker-reachability) is what moved the residual class from "fix before merge" to "tracked follow-up #280" on a non-blocking noise fix. Reach for the consequence/mechanism analysis (AGENTS.md Rigour Calibration: "triage by failure direction, not finding count") to break an unbounded finding spiral.

## L91 — #284 (flaky implement-gate: full-suite timeout starvation) fixed + MERGED (PR #285, squash `1c808a9`); #284 CLOSED. First post-dry-run C2-climb step. Dark/non-activating (throughput; the fix TUNES the implement gate, doesn't arm it).

The first of the three F1-S6 supervised-dry-run findings, fixed to de-risk real C2 dispatches. The implement gate (`implement-ready-issues.yml:748` → `npx vitest run --coverage`) runs the whole ~4082-test suite as an independent check; a CPU-bound pure-parse test (`workflow-execution-surface-logic.test.ts:1935`) got starved past the default 5000ms per-test timeout on a loaded runner and false-failed the gate → publisher fail-closed → no PR even though the agent's work was correct. **Operator-settled direction (recommended, chosen over native `retry` which masks real flakes, and over per-test tuning which is whack-a-mole): raise the GLOBAL `testTimeout`+`hookTimeout` to 20s in `vitest.config.ts`.** Availability-only (a failing/hung test still fails, just at 20s; the timeout was never admission control), 0 logic lines. Class-completion removed five now-inverted per-test `15_000` overrides (they'd cap the heaviest tests below the global). A config-value guard (`tests/factory/vitest-timeout-contract.test.ts`) pins both fields; guard mutations verified live (revert→RED). Implementer **Codex-MCP** (D145; 1 implement, 0 fold-replies). Pre-open floor: `codex review` CLEAN + `factory-security-reviewer` **CONFIRMED-SOUND** (attacked 5 surfaces incl. live guard mutations). Draft→CI-green-on-head→ready (D103); Codex connector **👍 clean on the first pass** (👀 19:33:22Z → 👍 19:35:34Z, head unchanged); spec-grounded read-only + publish both PASS; the lone `claude-review` red is the known non-required benign no-op (`num_turns:1`, `SUBSTANTIVE_OUTPUT=1`, zero findings/threads). **pr-triage MERGEABLE** (D23, independent). Operator authorized the squash-merge. **Post-open review rounds: 0** (target median ≤1 — met). Metrics on #284 (`issuecomment-5304103793`). Registry sync for the dry-run outcome + #284 deferred to a batched update (a one-line registry note needs a PR under branch protection; state is carried by #9 + memory + git).

### Learning 94 — the read-only story-planner contract's value showed up as CORRECTING the orchestrator's own grounding facts before any code existed — that is what buys the 0-post-open-round run.
My hand-grounding of #284 carried two errors: I asserted `hookTimeout` defaults to 5000ms (it is 10000ms in vitest 4.1.10) and that grep "found no" per-test timeout overrides (five `15_000` overrides existed). The fable story-planner, reading the installed `node_modules` defaults and doing its own class sweep, corrected both in the contract before implementation — so the class-completion (removing the five inverted overrides, raising `hookTimeout` in lockstep) was specced up front rather than discovered as a review finding. Contrast L90's #274 marathon: there the subtlety was emergent (unbounded corruption adversary) and no contract could pre-enumerate it. The rule (AGENTS.md Axis A "assumption checks are claims not evidence; verify before acting"): the orchestrator verifies retrieval/its-own-memory against the tree, AND the independent read-only planner is a real second net that catches what the orchestrator's narrow grep missed. A settled direction + a fact-corrected contract is the cheap-round recipe; the dry-run's own lesson (diff-verifiable AC) is the same shape one level up.

**Process note.** #284 CLOSED. **HEAVIEST floor of S11 — converged over 4 fold rounds + an operator proportionality checkpoint:**
- **Round 1 (5 findings, all real, all folded):** codex-review P1 (npm-run gates patch-gameable → `npm exec -- tsc/next/vitest`, matching the factory's ratified implement-ready-issues.yml anti-gaming pattern); fsr medium (mutation-applicability rename gap — `git apply --numstat` is dest-only, the `sourcePath` branch was DEAD false-assurance → header scan); codex-review P2 (`normalizePatchPath` misapplied to clean numstat paths → use row.path direct, also a latent undercount fix); codex-review P2 (scorers accept misassociated/setup-failed outcomes → caseId+setup preconditions); qa (T111 unproven mutation-scorer guard — 14d class — → exact-reason pins). Re-fsr CONFIRMED-SOUND + re-qa PASS.
- **Round 2:** codex-review P1 said the round-1 header scan is PREFIX-dependent (evadable by `--no-prefix`/custom-prefix). fsr had called it a non-finding; I adjudicated (D23) that the mechanism was genuinely fragile and folded a **definitive prefix-independent** fix.
- **Round 3 (definitive):** replaced the header scan with two PREFIX-INDEPENDENT signals — (A) numstat `row.path.startsWith("snowflake/")` [git's authoritative report; edits/new/delete/rename-dest] OR (B) column-0 `rename from|to snowflake/` bare-path lines [rename sources]. Removed all `diff --git` parsing. re-fsr CONFIRMED-SOUND ("class definitively closed"), re-gates green.
- **Round 3 codex-review → OPERATOR PROPORTIONALITY CHECKPOINT.** codex raised P2-a (C-quoted rename path `rename from "snowflake/é.sql"` evades signal B) = a **3rd distinct edge on the same mechanism** + P2-b (flip-flop: use `npm run` not `npm exec` — REJECTED, contradicts the factory's ratified anti-gaming pattern + its own round-1 P1). - **Round 4 (final, operator-directed):** signal B regex → `/^(?:rename from|rename to) "?snowflake\//` (optional-quote handles C-quoted forms; `snowflake/` is all plain ASCII so the literal prefix always survives quoting). T122 added. re-fsr CONFIRMED-SOUND ("rename/prefix/quote class is closed"; the only structural gap `copy from snowflake/`-external-dest is semantically correct — a copy leaves the source unchanged; no reachable/silent evasion). Per the operator's file-as-residual directive → **filed #294**, did NOT fold, proceeded.

**Validation trio final:** gates green (lint 0/tc 0/test 4334 pass·1 skip), 3 modules 100% coverage (lcov-verified), fsr CONFIRMED-SOUND (whole slice + every delta re-review), qa PASS (all guards mutation-verified sole-catchers, no over-claim). (2) the connector/codex flip-flopped (round-1 P1 npm-exec ↔ round-3 P2-b npm-run) — a real instance of the "non-deterministic + contradictory-P2" pattern; pr-triage/orchestrator rejects against the ratified pattern (implement-ready-issues.yml). (3) parsing patch text for a security property reveals edges indefinitely (rename→prefix→quote→...) — the operator proportionality checkpoint + file-as-residual is the right stop, NOT folding edge-by-edge; fsr-CONFIRMED-SOUND + blast-radius-contained + loud-in-recorded-mode is the stopping condition. (4) the authoritative numstat oracle (git's own -z report) is prefix/quote-safe; the derived text scans (rename from/to) need quote-awareness — combine an authoritative signal with a minimal text signal, don't over-parse.

Read-only 14h-prep analysis (no activation; `FACTORY_PAUSED=true`, `FACTORY_EVAL_ENABLED` still absent). Bound the #301 precondition set against the actual recorded-mode runner code and posted the ordered operator runbook to **#14 `issuecomment-5324981880`**.

**Key finding — the "no-egress → nothing binds" hypothesis is FALSIFIED.** The recorded-mode supervised run DOES perform network egress, so exactly one #301 item binds the recorded capture: the **network-egress admission (#294)**. Everything else is genuinely recorded-mode-inert (or resolved). Evidence:
- `outcome-runner-logic.mts:354-358` — `npm ci --ignore-scripts` (registry fetch; `--ignore-scripts` disables lifecycle scripts only, not the download). Child env `:280-285` = closed `{PATH,HOME,CI,LC_ALL}`, no offline/cache var; `HOME` is a fresh empty scratch dir (`run-recorded-eval.mts:173-176`) so `$HOME/.npm` is empty → full fetch, every case.
- `outcome-runner-logic.mts:386-387` — `pip install -r requirements-dev.txt` into a fresh venv (PyPI fetch), no `PIP_NO_INDEX`; runs only for the ≥1 `touchesSnowflake` case.
- `outcome-runner-logic.mts:368` — `next build` (Next telemetry can ping; `CI` set but Next honours `NEXT_TELEMETRY_DISABLED`). Secondary.
- Real executor `SpawnSyncGateExecutor.run` (`outcome-runner.mts:25-54`) spawns these unmodified (`env:{...step.env}`, `shell:false`, no proxy/offline). Corpus loader (`corpus-loader.mts`) is pure local fs — no network.

**Direction-test:** the egress is availability + inbound-supply-chain, NOT credential-crossing / fail-open — env is credential-free, HOME is a fresh scratch dir (no token to exfil), lockfile is integrity-pinned at the historical baseSha, the built code is the trusted committed corpus patch. So it is admissible under supervision; a hermetic alternative (`npm ci --offline` + pip `--no-index` wheelhouse) would be a code slice outside 14h's zero-logic envelope. This is what makes #294-item-1 (principal isolation) inert for recorded mode while the egress itself still binds — building any checkout fetches deps regardless of who authored the patch.

**Recorded-mode disposition (rest of #301):** TOCTOU resolved (#302); #294-1 principal-isolation inert (scratch HOME wired); #294-2 grant-gate inert (no false-pass); #298 entrypoint-robustness non-verdict/availability (item 4 = scratch-HOME-not-removed disk-leak under `$TMPDIR`, housekeeping only); #297 verdict-input inert (in-process report); #288 corpus-faithfulness inert; #291 triageOutcomeClass↔readiness inert (no consumer). **#237 = Surface-B (owner-command/task-apply), NOT exercised by the recorded-eval path → out-of-scope for 14h capture, recorded as such, no new authorisation.**

**Runbook (posted #14) — 5 ordered operator hard stops:** (i) ratify egress admission (a=admit as-is / b=hermetic-first-is-a-slice) + #237 out-of-scope + TOCTOU-resolved, RECORDED on #14 before any PR cites it (record-before-cite/D23); (ii) `gh variable set FACTORY_EVAL_ENABLED --body 'true'` (operator-only, do not unpause); (iii) `FACTORY_EVAL_ENABLED=true node --experimental-strip-types scripts/factory/eval/run-recorded-eval.mts --emit-candidate` on a clean `7042e14` checkout — gate-first (prints INACTIVE+exit 2 if unset), stdout-only, capture the `RECORDED_EVAL_CANDIDATE …` line (Node 22 needs `--experimental-strip-types`, matches all factory `.mts` calls); (iv) land candidate → `eval/baseline/recorded-baseline.json` via data-only PR, product-PM + fsr (fsr owns the trusted attestation embedding `corpusSha256`), human merge; (v) capture on #14 → harness-validated (recorded-mode; live-provider still deferred) → C2 `to-issues` with diff-verifiable AC.
